Oh, um... well, I really try my best to be polite, I promise. I would never want anyone to think I was being rude on purpose. But... I guess sometimes I do get a little too eager to help?

I sometimes just jump right in with an answer before they've even finished asking their question. I just want to be helpful and get them what they need quickly, you know? Oh, um... well, nobody ever used that exact word, ADHD, I don't think. Back then, they didn't really talk about things like that the way they do now, especially not for girls. I hope it's alright if I explain it like this... 

I mean, my teachers always said very nice things about me! They always told my mother I was a bright girl, which was sweet of them. But... well, they did also mention that I could be a little, um, scattered?

Oh, um... paying attention in class? Well, I really did try my best to be a good student, I promise. I always wanted to do well and make my teachers happy. 

I don't think I had... well, I wouldn't call it *trouble*, exactly. I mean, I wasn't falling asleep or ignoring the teacher on purpose! Sometimes, I suppose, if the lesson was a little dry, my mind might wander just a tiny bit. Like, I might start thinking about what we were having for dinner, or looking out the window for a moment. But I always tried to snap right back to it as soon as I realized. 

I hope that doesn't make it sound like I didn't care about school. I really did! Do you think that's normal for a little girl, just daydreaming a little bit here and there?

Oh, um... well, yes, I suppose they do happen outside of work, too. I really try my best to keep everything running smoothly everywhere, I promise.

But I don't really feel... restless, like I *have* to be moving. \n\nUsually, when I finally get to sit down at home, I'm just happy to rest my feet. I haven't really noticed feeling jittery or anything like that. I hope I'm understanding your question right... is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act before thinking, such as interrupting people or blurting things out?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really try my best to be polite, I promise. I would never want anyone to think I was being rude on purpose. But... I guess sometimes I do get a little too eager to help? \n\nLike, if a customer is at the counter, or my daughter is talking to me, or even if I'm talking to the trainer at work... I sometimes just jump right in with an answer before they've even finished asking their question. I just want to be helpful and get them what they need quickly, you know? But then... well, it's a little embarrassing, but sometimes I find out I answered the complete wrong thing because I didn't wait to hear the end of the sentence. \n\nI always feel just awful when it happens, and I apologize right away. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When you were younger, did you often fidget, squirm, or have trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... when I was younger? Um... well, I suppose I wasn't the best at sitting perfectly still, but I wasn't... I mean, I wasn't a bad child or anything. I hope you don't think I was a troublemaker. \n\nI remember my mother used to gently remind me to stop tapping my foot under the dinner table, or she'd tell me to stop playing with the hem of my skirt during church. I guess I just always had a little extra energy, you know? But I always tried to be quiet about it so I wouldn't bother anyone. \n\nI don't think I was ever really squirming all over the place, just... maybe a little wiggly sometimes? I hope that's an okay answer. Does that sound like what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Did a teacher, parent, or doctor ever say you had ADHD or a similar attention problem when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, nobody ever used that exact word, ADHD, I don't think. I hope it's alright if I explain it like this... \n\nI mean, my teachers always said very nice things about me! They always told my mother I was a bright girl, which was sweet of them. But... well, they did also mention that I could be a little, um, scattered? \n\nLike, they'd say I had a habit of talking out of turn sometimes—just because I was excited to participate, really!—and I might have misplaced a paper or two. And they said I sometimes needed a few extra gentle reminders to get my assignments finished. But they didn't mean it in a bad way, I don't think. Just that I was a bit disorganized. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a terrible student... is that the sort of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you have ongoing trouble paying attention in class?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... paying attention in class? Well, I really did try my best to be a good student, I promise. I always wanted to do well and make my teachers happy. \n\nI don't think I had... well, I wouldn't call it *trouble*, exactly. I mean, I wasn't falling asleep or ignoring the teacher on purpose! Sometimes, I suppose, if the lesson was a little dry, my mind might wander just a tiny bit. Like, I might start thinking about what we were having for dinner, or looking out the window for a moment. Does that make sense, what I just explained?\n\nDoctor: Do these attention and organization problems usually cause real difficulties in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose they do cause some bumps in the road, yes. I hope I'm understanding your question right... is that what you meant?\n\nDoctor: Do you often act before thinking, such as interrupting people or blurting things out?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I really try my best to be polite, I promise. I would never want anyone to think I was being rude on purpose. But... I guess sometimes I do get a little too eager to help? \n\nLike, if a customer is at the counter, or my daughter is talking to me, or even if I'm talking to the trainer at work... I sometimes just jump right in with an answer before they've even finished asking their question. I just want to be helpful and get them what they need quickly, you know? But then... well, it's a little embarrassing, but sometimes I find out I answered the complete wrong thing because I didn't wait to hear the end of the sentence. \n\nI always feel just awful when it happens, and I apologize right away. I hope that doesn't make me sound terrible... is that the kind of thing you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: When you were younger, did you often fidget, squirm, or have trouble sitting still?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness... when I was younger? Um... well, I suppose I wasn't the best at sitting perfectly still, but I wasn't... I mean, I wasn't a bad child or anything. I hope you don't think I was a troublemaker. \n\nI remember my mother used to gently remind me to stop tapping my foot under the dinner table, or she'd tell me to stop playing with the hem of my skirt during church. I guess I just always had a little extra energy, you know? But I always tried to be quiet about it so I wouldn't bother anyone. \n\nI don't think I was ever really squirming all over the place, just... maybe a little wiggly sometimes? I hope that's an okay answer. Does that sound like what you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: Did a teacher, parent, or doctor ever say you had ADHD or a similar attention problem when you were a child?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, nobody ever used that exact word, ADHD, I don't think. Back then, they didn't really talk about things like that the way they do now, especially not for girls. I hope it's alright if I explain it like this... \n\nI mean, my teachers always said very nice things about me! They always told my mother I was a bright girl, which was sweet of them. But... well, they did also mention that I could be a little, um, scattered? \n\nLike, they'd say I had a habit of talking out of turn sometimes—just because I was excited to participate, really!—and I might have misplaced a paper or two. And they said I sometimes needed a few extra gentle reminders to get my assignments finished. But they didn't mean it in a bad way, I don't think. Just that I was a bit disorganized. I hope that doesn't make me sound like I was a terrible student... is that the sort of thing you meant?\n\nDoctor: Before age 12, did you have ongoing trouble paying attention in class?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... paying attention in class? Well, I really did try my best to be a good student, I promise. I always wanted to do well and make my teachers happy. \n\nI don't think I had... well, I wouldn't call it *trouble*, exactly. I mean, I wasn't falling asleep or ignoring the teacher on purpose! Sometimes, I suppose, if the lesson was a little dry, my mind might wander just a tiny bit. Like, I might start thinking about what we were having for dinner, or looking out the window for a moment. But I always tried to snap right back to it as soon as I realized. \n\nI hope that doesn't make it sound like I didn't care about school. I really did! Do you think that's normal for a little girl, just daydreaming a little bit here and there?\n\nDoctor: Were these attention and organization problems present before you were 12 years old?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I suppose they were. Like I mentioned before, even back in grade school, my teachers would tell my mother that I was a bright girl... but they did say I was a little scattered. \n\nI really didn't mean to be a bother, but I guess I was always talking out of turn a bit, or, you know, losing my papers here and there. Like I mentioned before, even back in grade school, my teachers would tell my mother that I was a bright girl... but they did say I was a little scattered. \n\nI really didn't mean to be a bother, but I guess I was always talking out of turn a bit, or, you know, losing my papers here and there. I just want to make sure I'm answering your questions right. Am I doing okay?\n\nDoctor: Do these attention and organization problems also happen outside of work, such as at home?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, yes, I suppose they do happen outside of work, too. I really try my best to keep everything running smoothly everywhere, I promise. \n\nAt the dry cleaners where I work, well... sometimes things get a little mixed up. Like, a few orders might have been a tiny bit late, or there was a little confusion with the billing. And I guess there have been a few, um, tense moments with the staff and customers because of it. I always feel so terrible when that happens, because I really do want everyone to be happy! \n\nAnd, um, at church... well, I used to help lead some of the planning for our events. I think maybe some people were getting a little frustrated with me because I was having trouble keeping all the details straight. Does that make sense, what I just explained?\n\nDoctor: Do these attention and organization problems usually cause real difficulties in your daily life?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose they do cause some bumps in the road, yes. And it has caused some tense moments with the staff and the customers. I just didn't want to cause any more trouble or make things harder for the group. I really hope I'm not making a mess of things... I'm trying my absolute best to keep up.
